# Verification cruxes and open items

Status: `research`, living register started 2026-09-04. Every entry is a
question the evidence has not yet settled at rank 1 (ADR-0018): a text
that does not agree with itself, a third-party implementation that
disagrees with the baseline engine, a parameter no reference read so far
supplies, or a convention that has to be chosen for the arithmetic to
terminate. An entry is closed by a citation, never by a vote; until then
the affected row is marked T or S and ships as `UNSUPPORTED (unsourced)`
or with the baseline engine's value as the documented default.

| # | item | what is known | what is missing | rank of the evidence | action |

| C1 | Shashtihayani dasha years | **closed at rank 1, 2026-09-25**: BPHS ch. 46 v. 41 reads गुरवर्कभूसुतानां च दशा दश दशाब्दकाः, ततः शशिज्ञशुक्रार्कपुत्रागूनां रसाब्दकाः — Jupiter, the Sun and Mars **ten** years each, the Moon, Mercury, Venus, Saturn and Rahu **six** (rasa), sixty in all. | C46 | The oracle for Western and Hellenistic results | ADR-0025 moved both into v1 on 2026-09-10, and every exit in this project is a gate against a recorded answer. The astronomy underneath them is already covered: tropical longitude is exact against Teimeris to `0.000000″` (`STATUS.md`, Phase 4's baseline positions), and the twenty-two house systems and forty-seven ayanamshas are catalogued and bounded. **What has no oracle is the doctrine above the astronomy** — orb tables per aspect and per school, which progressions and by which arc, whether a return chart is precession-corrected and whether it is relocated, the Egyptian against the Ptolemaic and Chaldean term boundaries, the day and night reversal of each lot, the loosing of the bond in zodiacal releasing, and almuten weights that differ between Ibn Ezra and Lilly | a corpus. The conformance repository is Vedic and records no Western result at all (`teistro-conformance` v0.1.1); Teimeris computes positions and does not hold doctrine; and the field's own implementations disagree with each other, so no one of them is rank 1 | rank 1 texts exist and are unread here (Ptolemy's *Tetrabiblos*, Vettius Valens, Dorotheus, Firmicus, Bonatti, Lilly); rank 2 is the implementations, which disagree; rank 3 is general knowledge, which is where the list above currently comes from | **this is the largest unknown in the plan and blocks Phase 7's Western exit, not its build.** Three things in order, each a falsification pass before code as the project's law requires: derive what the texts state where they enumerate (terms, lots and sect are enumerable and so are derivable, exactly as the nakshatra tables were); record a fixture set with a stated oracle and its disagreements measured rather than resolved, the way `variants/*--geocentric.json` records a before and an after; and register every school as a cited row with the unsourced ones refused under ADR-0018, not defaulted. Until a row is cited it ships `UNSUPPORTED (unsourced)`, which is the same contract a Vedic row gets. **Surveyed on 2026-09-11** ([`20-western-hellenistic-oracle.md`](20-western-hellenistic-oracle.md)): three of the six rank 1 texts are public domain and obtainable, Tetrabiblos carries all three term systems and says which it rejects, and every rank 2 implementation is refused as a dependency by `deny.toml` (flatlib rides on an AGPL Swiss Ephemeris, immanuel-python is AGPL). The survey's own finding is structural: **Valens' hundred-odd worked examples decouple the oracle from the ephemeris**, because a fixture can record his positions as *inputs* and the doctrine's output as the answer — which also disposes of the fact that his second-century charts fall outside the built-in ephemeris's 1800 to 2400 and would otherwise have made the whole Hellenistic set engine-only. **Step 1's terms measured on 2026-10-01** ([`terms-measured.md`](../../03-design/terms-measured.md)): the *Tetrabiblos*'s Egyptian table is well-formed and gives the totals it states, the Chaldean terms are a rule that reproduces every order and total the chapter states, and Ptolemy's own terms are not fixed by his rule (C208); Ptolemy's houses and exaltation signs are the catalogue's, and Lilly's exaltation degrees are not |

Five words are loose. **\"Benefics in angles/trines as malefic are in 3rd, 6th and 11th\"** (vv. 56 and 65): a thousand years for any chart with one benefic so placed would be most charts, so every benefic and every malefic is asked of. **\"In exaltation, own/friendly divisions\"** (v. 63): read in the rasi. **\"The ascendant lord is not strong\"** (v. 76): read as weak, so a chart without strengths does not answer. **\"If they are helped by others\"** (v. 78): read as a benefic's company or aspect. **The lagna lord friendly, neutral or inimical to the Sun** (vv. 71 to 73) and **the eighth lord friendly to the Sun** (v. 67) want the natural friendship of two grahas, read as the lord's regard for the Sun since the relation is not symmetric, and built as `natural-relation` on 2026-09-17 | the Sanskrit, or a second translation, for each | rank 1, one translation | Shipped as stated, each reading in its rule's note; vv. 57 and 58, which read Vaiseshikamsa grades, are not shipped. On the translator's chart as the SDK casts it, every graha's basic Pindayu years are his to a hundredth, the Moon's to a tenth, and his Sun's and Moon's reductions are reproduced; his 82.25-year total is not, his visible-half shares being graded |

Read literally, *aspected or associated* means by any other of the seven, and Tajika counts **eight of the twelve** sign relations as an aspect. So an unqualified planet needs all six others inside the four neutral houses at once: over the corpus's 2 159 annual charts the Moon managed it **once**, and Khallasara held 2 times in 25 908 matters. Over the corpus's 2 159 annual charts only **171 of 15 113** readings (1.1%) fall under five with no dignity, and both lords are weak in **6 of 22 009** judged matters; at a lower floor of ten that becomes 2 871. | C122 | What a unit of an annual dasha's year is | The Mudda and the Varsha Yogini divide a year into 360 "days" and Charak's Patyayini into 365. Charak (p. 44) defines the 360 as **solar days, each the Sun's motion through one degree**, and says they could be spread "proportionately" over the civil year, though it is "more convenient to round". The *Tajika Nilakanthi*'s commentary offers the saura year of 360 or the savana year of 365 d 15 gh 30 pa, uses 360 in its example, and dates each period by adding its days to the Sun's degree. Charak's printed end dates are month arithmetic and fit no clock to the day. His durations are exact and clock-free | nothing a text could add: both define the unit, and neither makes the others wrong | rank 2, two books | **built as a knob, 2026-09-24** (`annual-dashas.md`): `sun_degrees` (default, both books' definition; the year ends on the next return), `even` (Charak's proportional spread), `days(n)` (his printed durations, and the one clock with no ephemeris). Measured over 2 159 recorded years in `muntha-measured.md` §19: the even spread stands up to **3.90 days** from the Sun's clock, so the choice moves a period's end by days |

The Sun stands on the ecliptic, which meets the horizon at the Ascendant and Descendant, so wherever the zodiac rises in order the Sun above the ecliptic horizon, its geometric altitude above zero and houses 7 to 12 of a quadrant division are one rule to within the Sun's latitude (under 1″); inside the polar circles part of the zodiac rises backwards and only the altitude is still the horizon; whole-sign houses 7 to 12 part from it, and Valens's degrees exclude them. The modern reading is no Ptolemaic perfection before she leaves her sign; the rival reading is within the moieties of orb. His ship at sea (p. 165, recast) calls her void 4.3° before a trine that perfects in her sign, which neither reading reproduces; his "If Presbytery shall stand" (p. 439, recast) has her void "after a little" with Mars's square 11.9° ahead, beyond their moieties (10°), which the moieties reproduce and the sign's end does not; his "A Lady, if marry the Gentleman desired?" (p. 385, recast) prints her "a vac: ad ☍ ☉" with the Sun's opposition 8.2° ahead, inside their moieties (14¾°) but past her sign's end, which both readings reproduce and a moiety reading without the sign's end would not. Four more agree with both: the horse lost near Henley (p. 467) is void, and the long-life question (p. 135), the Parsonage (p. 437) and the escaped prisoner (p. 470) apply to the aspect he names, first in her sign. Of seven figures the moieties read six as he does and the sign's end five; only the ship defeats both | — | rank 1 read; all seven figures recast by the Swiss Ephemeris's Moshier series | both ship: `MoonCourse::void` (no perfection before the sign ends) and `void_by_moieties` (none yet within half of each orb, `ConsiderationRules::orbs_deg`, p. 107's table), each perfection with its `gap_deg` |

| C362 | The permutation p-value (`research.md` §1.2) | `(b + 1)/(m + 1)` counts the observed labelling as one member of the reference set (Phipson and Smyth 2010); `b/m`, the formula most hand-rolled studies print, understates by about `1/m` and can be zero. A permuted statistic within a relative 10⁻¹² of the observed one counts as reaching it, because two counts whose statistics agree in exact arithmetic can differ by a rounding. The Clopper–Pearson interval on `b` of `m` is the Monte Carlo spread. The two-sided statistic is the standardised count's distance from its mean, so the exact two-group p sums the hypergeometric over the counts at least that many deviations away; Fisher's two-sided p as R prints it sums the counts no more probable than the observed one, and a doubled tail is a third convention; the three agree for balanced margins and part when the margins are unbalanced | — | rank 2, the statistics literature | decided: as stated. The exact p is the hypergeometric of the statistic the permutations rank, so the two are comparable cell for cell (test 3); a reader who wants Fisher's p has the counts in every row |
| C363 | Max-T as the correction the answer leads with (§1.5) | Westfall–Young max-T (step-down, their algorithm 4.1) holds the familywise error rate from the same permutations and uses the dependence between predicates, which yogas that share conditions have in plenty, where Bonferroni and Holm pay for it as if the predicates were unrelated. Its step-down rests on subset pivotality, which a permutation of exchangeable labels gives; a reader who doubts that the labels are exchangeable inside a stratum has Holm, which holds under any dependence and can be checked by hand. BH assumes positive dependence and BY does not | a published study whose predicates violate subset pivotality under label permutation | rank 2, Westfall and Young (1993) | decided: no `corrections` knob. Every row carries max-T, Holm, Bonferroni, BH and BY in that order, and `underAlpha` answers each separately. A study's permutations are refused when they cannot reach the strictest of them, Bonferroni's `alpha / M`, and the hint is the smallest count the same check accepts |
| C364 | Which event shuffle a timed study uses (§1.4) | `EVENT_DATES` moves event dates among people: it keeps the calendar of events (the season, and the slow grahas every chart shares at a date) and changes each person's age at the event, which is what a dasha reads; it can pair an event with a person born after it, and `afterBirth` either refuses or draws only among people born before the event, which changes the reference set. `AGES_AT_EVENT` reads person *i* at its birth plus person *j*'s age: it keeps the ages and moves the events in the calendar, which matters for a transit. No astrological study read names its shuffle, and no statistical text prefers one in general | a published event study that names its null | rank 2, none astrological | decided: neither is a default. The shuffle is a required field of a timed study in the façade, the record and every binding, and the answer's input hash carries it. The build first defaulted to `EVENT_DATES`, which destroys the age structure of the only delivery built, the dasha |
| C365 | A uniform expectation for a one-group study (§1.3) | births are not uniform in clock time (the early-morning peak before induction, the schedules of obstetrics after it) nor over the year, and a graha near the Sun ties its sector to the hour and the date; a uniform 1/12 finds "effects" in any sample of ordinary births, which is the Gauquelin record's first lesson. The design allowed one for a predicate on signs alone, "because the Sun's sign is the date"; but births are seasonal and the Sun stays between 29 and 32 days in a sign, so a sign predicate is not uniform either. Gauquelin's control keeps each birth's date and place and takes another subject's clock time, which keeps both margins and breaks their pairing (Abell, Kurtz and Zelen 1983 accepted it) | — | rank 2, the Mars effect record | decided: no uniform expectation is offered, so there is nothing to refuse. `expected` takes only the recombined control; the local clock time moves, so a donor's 05:40 stays 05:40 on the recipient's own civil day, inside strata (a birth decade) when the study gives them. A predicate the hour barely moves (the Sun's sign, which changes only on an ingress day) reads its expectation close to its observation, and a study of the date margin needs a reference population, which is `compare` with a control group |
| C366 | When an uncertain birth is unstable for a predicate (`research.md` §1.7) | reading only the instant and the two edges misses a predicate that changes and changes back inside the window, and at 720 minutes either side the lagna returns to its place, so every lagna predicate read at the edges alone is called stable | — | method | decided and built: unstable if the value anywhere in the window differs from the instant's, read every `uncertaintyStepMinutes` (2 by default) out to both edges; the step is part of the request and so of its hash |
| C367 | Which null a transit study uses (§1.4) | a transit can be age-locked (returns) or calendar-locked (a sign, a station); life events are age-patterned and seasonal, so each shuffle fails one kind; the case-crossover design (Maclure, *Am. J. Epidemiol.* 133, 1991) with time-stratified referents (Janes, Sheppard and Lumley, *Epidemiology* 16, 2005) matches the person and holds age and calendar inside a window | a published astrological event study that names its null | rank 2, epidemiology | **built 2026-10-12**: no default (as C364); `SELF_MATCHED` with `referents {windowDays, stepDays}`, windows fixed from birth; CMH over persons, Poisson-binomial exact p, conditional odds ratio; the measured page holds it at its level |
| C368 | How a restricted pairing is drawn (§2.5 `after_birth`) | rejection is uniform but can fail for want of luck; eligibility by date is nested, a Ferrers board (Goldman, Joichi and White, *Proc. AMS* 52, 1975), so a sequential draw is exact and counts the reference set | — | rank 2, combinatorics | decided; the sampler built 2026-10-12 as `research/shuffle/2`, the default, with `/1` selectable for answers already published; a board no dates can make, or one with no pairing, is refused naming the event. Complete enumeration built the same day as `research/shuffle/3`, the default: a set of no more than `m` arrangements (labellings, pairings, a board's, each person's instants) is walked whole, `b/N` exact, `enumerated` in the answer |
| C369 | The statistic and the effect under strata (§§1.6, 2.5) | the shuffle was stratified and the standardisation and the effect were not, so a stratified test ranked by the crude count, which mixes the strata's prevalences back in (on a Simpson's table every stratum's ratio is 1 and the crude 0.59); the Cochran–Mantel–Haenszel statistic (Mantel and Haenszel 1959) is the standardisation under the stratified null and is the plain one with one stratum; Mantel–Haenszel pooled ratios with the Greenland–Robins (1985) and Robins–Breslow–Greenland (1986) variances are the standard summary, and Breslow–Day with Tarone's correction (1985) tests that one ratio fits | — | rank 2 | decided and built 2026-10-12: the CMH statistic under strata, the exact p as the convolution of the strata's hypergeometrics, the pooled ratios beside the crude ones and the homogeneity p, each held against statsmodels 0.15 on three strata and the exact p against an enumeration |

| C383 | How Phaladeepika ch. 14 vv. 10–11's disease figures read: whose luminary, which afflicter, which malefic, which watery signs | Sastri puts the Moon and the Sun in the twelfth and second together, afflicted by Saturn and Mars, Mars alone in the fifth, and the sixth and eighth lords themselves in the seventh and eighth; Ojha reads either luminary in either house under Saturn or Mars (one gives less, both more), any cruel graha in the fifth, and Venus alone in the seventh or eighth; the Sanskrit (*samandārānvitavīkṣite*, *sogre … randhrārināthānvite*, *tadvat … śukre ca*) names a cruel graha with the sixth or eighth lord, and the rest likewise; Ojha's watery signs come from Horasara, Phaladeepika's own ch. 1 v. 7 makes them Cancer, the latter half of Capricorn and Pisces | a commentary reading the compounds otherwise | rank 1 verse, two rank-1 glosses | **decided and built 2026-10-14**: either luminary in the second or twelfth, joined or aspected by Saturn or Mars; the third, the eleventh and Jupiter each so touched; a malefic with the sixth or eighth lord in the fifth, or in the seventh or eighth, and Venus in the malefic's place; ch. 1 v. 7's watery signs |

| C396 | How Jataka Parijata ch. 4 vv. 69 to 73 and 97 to 104 read, from the Sanskrit where Sastri's 1932 scan lacks the pages | the Chowkhamba print with Kapileshvara Shastri's commentary and a Hindi gloss (archive.org dli.ernet.496248, pp. 111–112 and 120–122, read off the page images). vv. 69–70 read a *krūra-ṣaṣṭyaṁśa* (a cruel D60 part, by ch. 1's table), not the shadvargas the v. 68 note said. v. 71: the lagna lord exceedingly strong, *saumyānvite tādṛśa-dṛṣṭi-yoge*, joined with benefics and so aspected, in a kendra, free of a malefic's aspect; the Hindi reads "joined or aspected". v. 72: *candraḥ sampūrṇa-gātras tu saumya-kṣetrāṁśago 'pi vā*, the full Moon, or (the commentary's *api*, even waning) the Moon in a benefic's sign or navamsha, the more so aspected by Venus; the Hindi joins the halves into one full Moon in a benefic's sign or navamsha. v. 73: any one of Jupiter, Venus and Mercury strong in a kendra, free of malefic aspect and conjunction. v. 97: the Sun, Jupiter and Mars in Saturn's navamshas or in the ninth in its own navamsha, strong, the strong Moon in the lagna: to the yuga's end. v. 98: Jupiter and Saturn in one navamsha in the ninth or the tenth, aspected by benefics, the Sun rising: a long-lived ascetic. v. 99 is BPHS ch. 43 v. 55's figure, which the print quotes from Saravali and the SDK already ships. v. 100: no malefic in the trines, no benefic in a kendra, none in the eighth: like an immortal. vv. 101–104 name vaiseshikamshas (Gopura, Simhasana, Paravata, Devaloka) | a print of the commentary ranked above this one, or Sastri's own pages | rank 2 commentary on a rank-1 text | **decided and built 2026-10-14**: vv. 71 (both conjunction and aspect, as the verse), 72 as one antidote, the full Moon in a benefic's sign or navamsha, as the Svalpa Jataka verse the commentary quotes and the Hindi gloss read it (the commentary's waning Moon there holds on most births and is not built), "full" being Phaladeepika ch. 19 v. 8's middle ten days of the month, 73, 97 (unlimited), 98 (long) and 100 (divine, the heading's "likeness to the gods"); Venus's aspect in v. 72 grades and is not asked. **v. 69 built 2026-10-14**: the eighth lord or Saturn in a cruel shashtyamsha, the eighth lord joined by a malefic (the commentary's reading); the cruel parts are the commentary's own ch. 1 table (Chowkhamba p. 23, read off the image: odd-sign parts 1, 2, 5, 7–10, 12, 16, 24, 30–36, 40, 41, 43–45, 49, 52, 53, 56 and 59 ashubha, even signs reversed), written as degree spans. vv. 105–106 (liberation) built under `loka`, v. 105's "three or four grahas" counting the seven. Not built: v. 70 (the twelfth and second houses in cruel parts, which a whole-sign house has no degree for) and v. 101 ("from Saturn to Mars", *śanyādi-bhauma-paryanta*, is three grahas in the order of the spheres or four in the weekdays', and the print says neither). **vv. 102–104 decided and built 2026-10-14** with a new `n-of` condition: each name is the commentary's count of the graha's own vargas of the dashavarga (*svakīya-pañcavarge* for Simhasana, and so on), a varga its own by JP ch. 1 vv. 44–46 (exaltation, moolatrikona, own sign, and vargottama in the navamsha), not BPHS ch. 6's, which adds the arudha lagna's kendra lords; the counts are exact, Devaloka seven or eight by ch. 1's ladder. v. 102's "end of Aries" rising is its last navamsha, the measure the verse gives the Moon; v. 103 asks only Jupiter in the lagna (both glosses); its "like a sage" is the divine class, v. 102's countless years and v. 104's yuga's end the unlimited. v. 103 never holds from 1800 to 2398 (Saturn 7–8 and Mars 6 meet on about thirty days, Jupiter then at most four) |
| C397 | Whether a significator's own co-significator or another party's significator prohibits | Lilly's "If I should purchase Master B. his houses" (*Christian Astrology* p. 219, 31 March 1634, recast at the printed Ascendant): Venus applies to Saturn's trine, Saturn retrograde, with "no abscission or frustration ere the perfect aspect" (p. 220). Before Venus perfects, the Moon squares Saturn (4 hours) and the Sun trines him (16 hours), each p. 111's prohibition by aspect. Lilly reads the Moon as "translating the influence of ♂" to Saturn and the Sun, lord of the seventh and of the eleventh, "in perfect △ with ♄", both as arguments for, and he bought the houses. The Moon is the querent's co-significator and the Sun the seller's significator; his p. 385 Lady figure also reads the Moon's contacts as no prohibition, though all lie past her sign (C234) | — | rank 1 read on the page images (pp. 219–220), the figure recast | open: two figures, and the Sun's case rests on one; every contact is still reported as a prohibition, and the test pins both (`lillys_houses_figure_applies_against_a_retrograde_and_nothing_frustrates`) |
| C398 | Which muhurtas of each weekday are censured (durmuhurta) | Muhurta Chintamani, *Vivaha Prakarana* v. 52 (1906 ed., p. 89, leaf n105, page image), names deities over the day list of v. 50 (Girisha, Bhujaga, Mitra, Pitrya, Vasu, Ambu, Vishve, Abhijit, Vidhata, Indra, Indragni, Nirriti, Udakanatha, Aryaman, Bhaga) and the night list of v. 51 (Shiva, Ajapada, Ahirbudhnya, Pusha, Ashvi, Yama, Agni, Brahma, Chandra, Aditi, Jiva, Vishnu, Arka, Tvashtra, Maruta): Sunday Aryaman; Monday ब्राह्म and रक्षः; Tuesday वह्नि and पित्र्य; Wednesday Abhijit; Thursday तोय and रक्षः; Friday ब्राह्म and पित्र्य; Saturday ईश and सार्प. Agni is only in the night list, so Tuesday's is the night's 7th; Brahma (day 9 Vidhata, night 8), water (day 6 Ambu, day 13 Udakanatha) and Shiva (day 1, night 1) are each in two places, and the edition's Hindi reads every one as the daylight's: Monday 9 and 12, Thursday 6 and 12, Friday 4 and 9, Saturday 1 and 2. Raman's *Muhurtha* (ch. V, p. 32, page image) numbers his own day list, which has no Abhijit and puts Vidhi 8th and Vahni 11th: Monday 8 and 12, Tuesday 4 and 11, Thursday 12 and 13 (Varuna), Friday 4 and 8, all of the daylight, and Wednesday "abhijit" without a number | the *Piyushadhara* commentary's reading of the two-place names, which would settle Thursday's water and Monday's Brahma | rank 1 against rank 2 | `panchanga.durmuhurta`: `MUHURTA_CHINTAMANI` (the verse with its edition's Hindi) in the root profile, `RAMAN` as printed (`03-design/panchanga-day.md` §21.1) |

| C413 | What a chain of dispositors and a final dispositor are | A planet's dispositor is "the ruler of the sign it is in" (Leo, *How to Judge a Nativity*, 1928 ed., p. 130, the Kaiser's Moon in Scorpio disposed by Mars). Neither text follows a dispositor's own dispositor, and neither names a final dispositor or a chain. Both are 20th-century practice, and no text defining them has been read | a text that defines the final dispositor, with whether a cycle or two planets in their own signs can still yield one | rank 1 for the dispositor itself (Lilly, Leo); none read for the chain and its end, so rank 3 at best (modern practice) | shipped as the one definition applied again (`teistro_hellenistic::dispositions`, `essential-dignities.md` §Dispositors). A chain stops at a planet in its own sign or at a repeat. A final dispositor exists only when every chain stops at the same planet in its own sign |
| C414 | Which rulers a dispositor is read under | the traditional seven's houses are the catalogue's sign lords (Lilly's table, p. 104), which the dignities already read. Leo has Uranus *share* Aquarius with Saturn (p. 103) and calls Pisces "usually regarded as the house of Neptune" (p. 126). Pluto was found after Leo, and its rule of Scorpio is modern practice. A chain needs one lord a sign, and Leo's sharing gives two | a source for Pluto's rulership and for replacing rather than sharing the old lord | rank 1 for the traditional, rank 2 (Leo) for Uranus and Neptune, rank 3 for Pluto | two rows, `Rulers::Traditional` (the default, the one `Dignities` carries) and `Rulers::Modern`, which gives Aquarius, Pisces and Scorpio to Uranus, Neptune and Pluto alone. Leo's co-rule is not a scheme. Every ruler named must be given, so a modern reading without the outer planets is refused, not half-read |
| C415 | How a chain that never reaches a planet in its own sign is reported | each planet has one dispositor, so every chain ends at a planet in its own sign or enters a cycle. A cycle of two is a mutual reception by house (Leo p. 249n). Some practice picks a winner inside a cycle (the strongest, or calls the pair "co-final"). No source read does so | a source that resolves a cycle | none read | the cycle is reported whole as `ChainEnd::Cycle`, each member in the next one's sign, starting at its slowest member (Pluto, Neptune and Uranus, then the Chaldean seven), so a cycle has one spelling whatever order the bodies come in. It is never a final dispositor, even when every chain ends in the same cycle. `Dispositions::ends` lists each end once, so a reader who resolves cycles has what they need |
| C416 | Whether the nodes take part in dispositor chains | the nodes rule no sign in Lilly's table or Leo's, so they are nobody's dispositor. Some later Vedic practice gives Rahu and Ketu co-lordship of Aquarius and Scorpio, which no shipped table carries | a Western source that follows a node's chain | none read | the nodes are refused by `dispositions` (`planets[k]`), and the façade leaves them out. House overlays are later practice, and no text defining them has been read. So which division the other chart's houses are, whether a point on a cusp is in the house it opens or the one it closes, and whether Lilly's 5° before a cusp or Leo's hour above the ascendant widens a house, are all open | a text that reads a planet in another chart's house, with its division and its cusp rule | none read (rank 3 practice at best) | built from the single chart's decisions (`western-houses.md` decisions 1 and 2, `western-synastry.md` §House overlays): the other chart's own Western cusps, under the division the `HouseRequest` names, else the profile's, else Placidus. A point is counted by the cusps alone, and a point on a cusp is in the house it opens. Neither Lilly's 5° nor Leo's reach is applied. Whole signs are one more division. Each side's division is reported, since a polar policy can fall back for one chart only |
| C418 | What a varga ingress is | the research row (`11-transits-gochar.md`) names JHora's navamsha and varga change charts; no text read defines a transit's entry into a divisional sign. They differ where two neighbouring parts send a body to one sign: the hora at every sign's end (Cancer or Leo on both sides, so it changes only at mid-sign) and the like in other charts | which line is the event | none read | the **sign change**, as `vargas::change::changes` has always reported a body's divisional changes; the navamsha the default chart, the rashi refused (it is `SIGN_INGRESS`); `transit-hit-list.md` §7.1 |

The eight-phase set would be the same search on a 45° lattice, not built; `transit-hit-list.md` §7.2 |

| C423 | What a "peak" of zodiacal releasing is, and whether it is scored | Valens, *Anthologies* II.17K (79K): the Lot of Fortune "will be equivalent to the Ascendant", the tenth from it "to MC" ("Rank"), the seventh the Descendant, the fourth the IC. IV.7 (165K–166K) judges a releasing period by whether the transmitting and receiving signs "are at an angle or are preceding an angle", and says a distribution "to MC (MC relative to the Lot of Fortune) or to the Lot itself" brings rank. The word "peak" and the ranking with the tenth first come from later practice (Brennan, *Hellenistic Astrology*, 2017, the zodiacal releasing chapter; paraphrased from memory, not checked here) | a rank-1 text that weighs the four angles against each other | rank 1 read (Riley's translation, Book II and IV) for the angles; rank 3 for "peak" | the reading chosen: a period's sign is judged by its whole-sign angle from the lot, so `releasing_angle(lot, sign)` reports `First`, `Fourth`, `Seventh` or `Tenth`, and nothing for any other sign. No score ships. The rival, a ranked peak score with the tenth strongest, is later practice and is left to a consumer. `hellenistic-time-lords.md` §Peaks |
| C424 | Which lot the angles are counted from when the releasing starts from Daimon | IV.7 (166K), inside the discussion of Daimon's distribution for rank: "from Daimon to MC (MC relative to the Lot of Fortune) or to the Lot itself". IV.8's Daimon example (Scorpio) names no angle of a period | a worked Daimon nativity that names a period's angle | rank 1 read | the reading chosen: from Fortune, for `RELEASING_FORTUNE` and `RELEASING_DAIMON` alike. The rival counts from the lot the releasing starts from (Daimon itself), which Valens's parenthesis does not support. The reader takes the lot as an argument, so either reading can be passed. That is also why it is a free function: the kernel releasing from Daimon does not know Fortune's sign. Later practice also counts from Fortune for both (Brennan, as C423, not checked here) |
| C425 | What "the places square with the Lots are most efficacious" ranks | IV.7 (166K): a distribution "at the Ascendant, at MC, or at the other angles forecasts high rank, but not to the same extent, because the places square with the Lots are most efficacious". II.17K calls all four of the lot's angles "the points square with it" | a second passage using "square with the Lots" | rank 1 read; the rival is also a reading of it | the reading chosen: the lots' angles, all four, outrank the Ascendant's, because II.17K uses "square" for the whole set of angles. The rival reading takes "square" as 90° only, so that the fourth and tenth outrank the first and seventh. Neither reading is encoded: the angle is reported and the weighing is left to the consumer. A consumer who wants the Ascendant's angles passes the Ascendant's sign |

| C427 | Which periods are "loosed": only the jump to the opposite sign, or every period after it | IV.4 (162K): after "17 years 7 months" "we will allot the remaining time using the signs in opposition". IV.10 (171K): "begin the remaining days starting with the sign in opposition". IV.5 (163K) treats the transmission made "after <one> cycle" as a moment with its own effect | a text that names the later periods in the remainder as loosed | rank 1 read | both readings ship as separate answers. `ReleasingDasha::loosing(parent)` is the jump itself: the thirteenth child, at 211 units, in the sign opposite the parent's. `ReleasingDasha::is_loosed(period)` is true for that child and every child after it, which is the reading chosen for "loosed". The rival reading, the jump alone, is `loosing`. Valens's cycle of days is 528 and the kernel's is 527½ (211 × 2½); his 528 is read as a rounding, as is his 44 days for the fourth level's 211 × 5 hours (43 days 23 hours). No number moves |
| C428 | Which text is gochar's Sarvatobhadra, and where Abhijit stands in it | `gochar.md` §1 named Phaladeepika ch. 26 vv. 26–28 as "the 28-star Sarvatobhadra vedha". Read on Sastri's 1950 page images (pp. 296–298), v. 26 is the seven-line *saptashalaka* (रेखाः सप्त…), the Sun's vedha of the janma, adhana and karma stars, v. 27 the other grahas' vedha of them, and v. 28 the taras. The Sarvatobhadra is v. 48 (p. 301): its benefic vedhas bring good and its malefic ones sorrow, to be weighed in gochar. The chakra itself is *Narapati Jayacharya Svarodaya*'s (1946 Khemraj print, pp. 55–62, leaves n64–n71, read on the images), whose vv. 2–9 build it and whose p. 58 prints it. Sastri prints the same verses under v. 48 (pp. 305–307) from *Jatakabharana* and *Horaratna*. Rival: none for the chakra. The grid gives Abhijit a cell, and a transit needs a span: none of these texts gives one | a text that spans Abhijit for a transit | rank 1 read (Phaladeepika, *Svarodaya*); the Abhijit span is C292's | **decided and built 2026-10-17**: the chakra is *Svarodaya*'s, generated from vv. 2–9 and held to the print cell for cell; a graha over 276°40′ to 280°53′20″ stands in Abhijit, C292's span; the saptashalaka and the taras are not built (`gochar.md` §8) |
| C429 | What a graha's front strikes, and which diagonal is its left | *Svarodaya* v. 13: from its star a graha strikes along three lines, left, front and right, by its sight. Every worked example lists the front's far star alone and every cell of both diagonals: *Jatakabharana* vv. 4–6 (Krittika, Rohini, Mrigashira, nine cells each), Rajavijaya (p. 306, with the prose "in the left or the right, the star, letter, sign and vowel, and not the others"), and Ranahasti in the tika on vv. 15–18, which reports it as "some teachers'". Rival: the front strikes every cell it passes, v. 13 alone. Left and right are not defined. Facing the centre from its star, a graha's right runs back to the stars before its own on every side, so the retrograde look back (v. 15), and an observer fixed facing east cannot tell the two diagonals apart on the north and south sides | a text that defines left and right | rank 1 read; the examples rank 1 as quoted by a rank-2 editor | **decided**: the front strikes its far star only, as all three examples print it, with `FrontVedha::EveryCell` for the rival. Left and right are as the graha sees them facing the centre. The examples are a test, cell for cell |
| C430 | Which lines a graha looks along, and its motion | v. 15: the five from Mars look right when retrograde, left when fast and ahead when mean. v. 19: Rahu and Ketu are always retrograde and the Sun and the Moon always fast, "and so, their motion being of one nature, always the three sights" (*Horaratna* v. 14 reads alike). The tika reports "some": as the motion, so the vedha, the Sun and the Moon to the left and the nodes to the right. vv. 16–18 give the motion "without computing the grahas", by the Sun's sign counted from the graha's (ग्रहाद्द्वितीयगे सूर्ये): 2nd, 11th and 12th fast; 3rd even and 4th slow; 5th–6th retrograde, 7th–8th very, 9th–10th crooked; with the Sun, unseen. The tika counts the other way, the graha from the Sun | a line for "slow" and for the unseen; a measure of "fast" from an ephemeris | rank 1 read | **decided**: the five look along one line by their motion, and the Sun, the Moon and the nodes along all three, v. 19 read whole; `Sight::OneEach` and `Sight::AllThree` are knobs. The kernel takes the five's motion from its caller. `Motion::by_the_sun` gives vv. 16–18's rule counted as the verse counts. "Slow" is read as mean (neither fast nor retrograde), and the unseen as no motion (`None`). Measured on a sky that can happen: by that rule Mercury is never retrograde and Venus at most mean. The façade passes the **sky's** by default: retrograde when the transit chart's speed is negative, fast above the graha's mean daily motion (the *Surya Siddhanta*'s revolutions, I.29 to 34, over I.37's civil days; Mercury's and Venus's the Sun's), mean otherwise, which takes in the slow as the tika defines it, "less than the even and the mean"; the tika introduces vv. 16–18 as the motion "without computing the grahas" (ग्रहगणितं विना, p. 57), so the computed motion is what the rule stands in for. `SarvatobhadraMotion::Sun` is vv. 16–18's rule, and the unseen graha, to which the verses give no motion, takes the sky's; that is where an inferior planet's retrograde falls. `Given` is the caller's. v. 20's "fast" says the fast keep their own nature, not that an unseen graha is fast (`gochar.md` §8.5) |
| C431 | Which of the native's points are struck, and how a letter or vowel is | v. 28: the star, the letter, the vowel, the tithi and the sign, each with its effect, and five struck kill. The tika on v. 27 reads them as the name's ("नाम्नः स्वरवर्णराशिनक्षत्र"). *Svara Chintamani* (quoted p. 307) adds the birth weekday. v. 21: the vowels two by two; v. 22: b–v, ś–s, ṣ–kh, ṅ–ñ, where *Horaratna*'s print (p. 306) reads a fifth pair, ja–ya, at *Svarodaya*'s ज्ञेयौ. v. 23: the triples gha ṅa cha, ṣa ṇa ṭha, dha pha ḍha and tha jha ña struck with ka, pa, bha and da. v. 24: struck by a graha in Ardra, Hasta, Purvashadha and Uttarabhadrapada. v. 26: a graha in the last pada of the star before a corner or the first of the one after strikes "the four from a" and Purna. The tika's tradition reads these as the corner's diagonal (a u ḷ o at the north-east), its "other explanation" as the four outermost vowels a ā i ī, which no line reaches. Sastri renders the first | the native's vowel for a name the caller does not give; a text that settles ja–ya | rank 1 read | **decided**: the five and the weekday, from the star and sign the caller passes (the birth's, or the name's where the birth is unknown); v. 22's four pairs (*Svarodaya*'s print), v. 23's pillars and v. 24's stars, all of them; a name beginning with a vowel at the letter a, as the śatapada heads its vowels' row (C291); a nukta's letter as its base; the corner's diagonal, `CornerVedha::OuterVowel` the rival. A letter the chakra does not read is refused as `native.name.letter` |

The Moon wanes from 180° of elongation, the dark half. Any benefic in a malefic's sign is malefic (v. 30 widening v. 12), the malefics named on the reading (`joined_by`), so a reader can undo it. The reading lists malefic and benefic strikers per point and scores nothing |

| C468 | What Phaladeepika's "auspicious bhava" is | ch. 6 v. 37 places the lords of Raja and Sankha together in an auspicious house without listing it | the list | rank 1, silent | **any house but the sixth, eighth and twelfth**, the two lords different grahas; v. 44's "good houses" read the same |
| C469 | What the twelve good house yogas ask | ch. 6 v. 44: a house joined or aspected by benefics, its lord in a good house or bright in its own sign or exaltation; Sastri joins the lord's two clauses by "or", and the Sanskrit's instrumental plural may give the dignity to the benefics instead | a second commentary | rank 1 with its translation | **the translation's reading**: a benefic in or aspecting the house, and its lord outside the sixth, eighth and twelfth or uncombust in its own sign or exaltation |
| C470 | What the twelve ill house yogas ask | ch. 6 v. 57: the house's lord in the sixth, eighth or twelfth, or the house joined or aspected by malefics; the second clause alone holds of nearly every house on nearly every chart, since Saturn, Mars and the nodes see most of the twelve | a commentary narrowing the second clause | rank 1, the verse | **the lord in the sixth, eighth or twelfth**, which is how Harsha, Sarala and Vimala, the sixth, eighth and twelfth's, are known; the malefic clause is not asked |
| C471 | Whether v. 70's figures can hold | Phaladeepika ch. 6 v. 70 asks every lord of the sixth, eighth and twelfth strong in a kendra or trikona and every lord of the first, fourth, ninth and tenth weak or combust in the sixth, eighth or twelfth, or the reverse; a graha ruling houses of both sets would have to stand in both places | the verse's own exception | rank 1, the arithmetic | **built as the verse says**, each lord asked; only Leo, Virgo, Sagittarius and Pisces rising give the two sets seven different grahas, so on the other eight lagnas neither figure holds |
| C472 | What ch. 7's two figures of named signs ask | Phaladeepika ch. 7 v. 10 puts the Sun "in the centre" of Sagittarius with the Moon there, Saturn rising and Mars of much power exalted; v. 15 puts Venus, Jupiter and Saturn in Pisces and the Moon "almost full" exalted with Aries rising; a full Moon in Taurus has the Sun near Scorpio, more than Venus's 47° from Pisces | the verses, and the sky | rank 1, the arithmetic | **built** with the signs the verses name; v. 10's centre and power and v. 15's fullness are not asked, since the first two have no measure in the verse and the last cannot join Venus in Pisces |
| C473 | Whom ch. 7's plural classes mean | v. 16 asks "the benefics" debilitated or in enemies' signs in the 3rd, 6th or 11th, or at highest exaltation, or all in kendras with bright rays, at a night birth with the Moon in Cancer in the tenth; v. 24 asks "malefics" in the 3rd, 6th and 11th from the lagna lord's or the Moon sign lord's sign | the verses | rank 1 | **built** as each of the named grahas: v. 16's benefics are Mercury, Jupiter and Venus, the Moon being placed apart, and each of the three must meet the same one of the three states; v. 24's malefics are the Sun, Mars and Saturn, each in one of the three houses from either lord |
| C474 | What ch. 7 v. 19's Moon stands in | the Moon "in a water-resorting rasi or amsa identical with the lagna, or in her own or a benefic's varga"; the second half takes the same Moon away from the kendras with no malefic in one | ch. 1's sign natures, and the verse | rank 1 | **built** with ch. 1's watery signs, Cancer, Pisces and the latter half of Capricorn (the navamsha in any of the three, since a navamsha has no halves), her own varga as Cancer or her exaltation, and a benefic's as a sign of Mercury, Jupiter or Venus; the first figure asks her in the lagna, the second outside every kendra |
| C475 | What Brihat Jataka ch. 11's loose figures ask | v. 14 has a second reading with the Sun in Leo; v. 16's "the other planets" in six houses, which the commentator reads as one or more of them, each in one of the houses; v. 20 puts Jupiter, Venus and Mercury in the lagna with the Sun in the tenth, which Iyer's note shows the inferior planets' elongations forbid, and asks "powerful benefic signs" in the kendras | Iyer's notes and Bhattotpala as he quotes him | rank 1, with the translator's notes | **built** as v. 14's first reading; v. 16's Sun, Moon, Mars and Saturn each in one of the six houses with Jupiter or Venus in the ninth; v. 20's first figure as the note's proposal, a sign of Jupiter, Venus or Mercury rising, and its second as every kendra a benefic's sign, which only a dual sign rising gives, with the Sun, Mars and Saturn each in a malefic's sign |
| C476 | What Brihat Jataka ch. 23's evil yogas leave unsaid | v. 1 tends the fifth and seventh "by benefic planets or by their lords"; vv. 10, 11 and 16 put "the malefic planets" in four houses "in any way"; v. 3's house list reads "the Toll, the 9th and the 5th" in the scan; v. 14 counts the lord of the Moon's navamsha among four grahas | Iyer's notes, and the scan | rank 1, with the translator's notes | **built** with the lord in its own house or aspecting it as tending it; the malefics as each of the grahas the notes name (the Sun, the Moon, Mars and Saturn in vv. 10 and 11, the Sun, Mars and Saturn in v. 16), each in one of the houses; v. 3's first house as the seventh; v. 14 is not built, since a count over bodies cannot hold a lord whose body changes with the chart, nor v. 16's drekkana figure, v. 6's painter, v. 5's male and female planets together or v. 11's teeth, whose readings the notes leave open |
| C477 | What the month's dasha reads for "the sign of the two lords" and for "days" | *Tajika Nilakanthi*, Varsha Tantra, v. 14: *inthihālagnapayoḥ rāśir yo balī tatra haddapāḥ / daśeśāḥ svāṃśatulyāhair*; the gloss reads the pair as the Muntha's lord and the month lagna's lord, and does not say whether a lord's sign is where it stands or what it rules, nor how a degree is a day; the year's dasha counts the Sun's degree as a day (C122), and the Dina Pravesha opens a day at each degree | the gloss, the chapter's own clock | rank 1, the verse and its gloss | **built** as the sign the stronger lord stands in, the stronger by Vishwa bala, a tie the Muntha's lord as the verse names it first, and a day the Sun's degree from the month's opening, so the five Huddas end as the next month opens; the sign it rules (`OWNED`), an even share of the month (`EVEN`) and thirty civil days (`DAYS`) are named readings |
| C478 | What Saravali ch. 35's raja yogas leave unsaid | v. 8's second line puts Jupiter "with Mars" after the first line exalts him; v. 16 places the exalted Sun and the Moon without naming a house; v. 26's first half joins the kendras' benefics to Saturn, the Sun and Mars in a compound the print does not settle; v. 32's "benefics in the kendras" has no count; v. 31 asks a full Moon in Cancer with every other graha in a shirshodaya sign, which puts the Sun opposite her in Capricorn; v. 85's Venus in Libra aspects a Moon in Taurus, six signs on | the 1907 Nirnaya Sagar print, read off the page images | rank 1, the verse | **built** with v. 8 as one figure restated, v. 16 in the lagna as v. 15's figure stands, v. 26's second half alone, and v. 32 as one benefic or more; v. 31 and v. 85 are not built, the first holding on no sky and the second asking an aspect graha drishti does not cast. The verses restating Brihat Jataka ch. 11 (vv. 5, 6, 11 to 14, 18, 19, 23, 25) and the ones Phaladeepika ch. 7 takes over (vv. 57, 59 to 62, 66, 68, 75) ship once, under the text that already carries them; vv. 33, 45, 50 and 80 ask what no sky holds (every graha deeply exalted, six exalted, Mercury and Venus aspected by the Sun from the tenth); vv. 90 to 92 and 94 are nabhasa figures already shipped; the rest of vv. 35 to 100 name no placement a rule can hold. Of vv. 103 to 163, vv. 103 to 105, 107, 108, 112, 118, 131 and 151 restate Brihat Jataka ch. 11 or Phaladeepika ch. 7 and v. 144 repeats v. 58 word for word; v. 128's "taraṇeś ca nandane" is read as the Sun in the fifth, v. 139's Venus as aspecting Jupiter, since Venus casts only the seventh aspect and never stands seventh from Mercury, and v. 147's start, middle and end of a sign as its thirds; v. 130 asks a full Moon in Taurus with the Sun in Aries, and vv. 125, 132, 141 to 143, 156, 161 and 162 do not settle whose placement they name. Of vv. 164 to 186, v. 164's Krittika is never vargottama, since only Ashvini's first quarter is, so the Moon's rule holds in Ashvini alone; v. 170's four places are read from the lagna in the order named and its "enemy's sign" as the chart's dignity; v. 183's watery signs are Cancer, Scorpio and Pisces; and v. 185's five friendly grahas as any five. Verses 169, 173, 179, 181 and 186 ask Mercury or Venus to stand where their elongation never reaches (the Sun third from Mercury, Mercury third or eleventh from the Sun, Mercury aspecting Venus, the Sun aspecting Mercury, Venus three signs from the Sun), and vv. 180 and 184 ask Jupiter to aspect two signs no single sign of his aspects both; v. 168's "from the retrograde" names no graha, v. 177 restates the Sunapha family from the kendras and v. 178 is Phaladeepika ch. 7's |
| C479 | Which set Saravali ch. 18 v. 13 reads | Saravali chs. 15 to 19 read every set of two to six of the seven grahas sharing a sign, a verse to a set, and each verse names its grahas. Ch. 18 prints twenty-two verses for its twenty-one sets of five: v. 13 names Mars, the Moon, Mercury, Venus and Saturn, the five v. 20 names, while vv. 1 to 12 and 14 to 22 cover all twenty-one between them. Chs. 15 to 17 and 19 keep strict combinatorial order; ch. 18 departs from it at vv. 13 to 16. Chs. 16 vv. 36 to 38 and 19 v. 8 speak of sets in general (malefics with a luminary, benefics together, three malefics, five or six together) | the 1907 Nirnaya Sagar print, read off the page images (pp. 41 to 51) | rank 1, the verse | **built** one rule a set, `SARAVALI_TOGETHER_*`, with v. 20 at the set's place in the run; v. 13 is not built, its reading being a second one for a set already read and its place the one a set of the Sun, Mars, Mercury, Venus and Saturn would take, which v. 14 reads. A set fires whenever its grahas share a sign, as Jataka Parijata's do, so three grahas in a sign also answer their pairs. The general verses grade or restate sets already read and are not built; ch. 19 v. 8 ships already as `SARAVALI_FIVE_OR_SIX_GRAHAS_TOGETHER` |
| C480 | What Saravali chs. 13 and 14 leave to the reader | Ch. 13 v. 1 names Sunapha, Anapha and Durudhara by grahas other than the Sun in the second from the Moon, the twelfth and both, "in order"; ch. 14 v. 1 names veshi, voshi and ubhayachari likewise from the Sun, the Moon excepted. Neither names a node. Ch. 13 v. 2 makes Kemadruma where none of the three holds and the Moon has no graha in a kendra, and very hard where no graha aspects her, without saying whose kendras; vv. 30 to 32 read the Moon's place from the Sun, her being seen by day or night, and benefics in the upachayas | the 1907 Nirnaya Sagar print, read off the page images (pp. 37 to 41) | rank 1, the verse | **built** each yoga as excluding the next, Sunapha with no graha in the twelfth and Anapha with none in the second, as Phaladeepika's ship, over Mars to Saturn; the graha-by-graha readings ask the same of the rest, so the general yoga holds exactly where one of them does, which a test holds over the corpus. Kemadruma is not built, its kendras having no reference; v. 30 restates BPHS ch. 37 v. 1, which ships; v. 32 grades wealth by the count of benefics in the upachayas from the lagna and, a degree less, from the Moon, a grading the SDK ships from the Moon alone in BPHS ch. 37 v. 6's form; and v. 31 weighs by the Moon's being seen, which the kernel does not read |
| C481 | What Jataka Parijata ch. 7 vv. 128 to 163 leave to the reader | The named yogas Parvata to Hariharabrahma. v. 130 names the guru-bandhu lords for Kahala, which the translation, the Chowkhamba commentary and Jatakadesha's parallel read as the ninth and fourth and BPHS as Jupiter and the fourth; v. 146's lagna-dharma for Matsya, which the translation reads as the ninth from the lagna; v. 152's parama-uccha, deep exaltation, which the verse sets on the ninth lord and the translation on the lagna lord; v. 154's trikona shubhetara, which the translation reads of the Moon; v. 162's houses, named beside the grahas with no "respectively"; and the translation's pp. 456 and 457, which hold vv. 134 to 136, Malika from the fifth house on and Chamara's definition | V. Subrahmanya Sastri's 1932 translation, rendered off the page images (pp. 453 to 468), and the Internet Archive's second upload of it, which lacks pp. 456 and 457 too; vv. 134 to 136 and the commentary on vv. 130, 152 and 154 from the Chowkhamba print with Kapileshvara Shastri's commentary and a Hindi gloss (archive.org dli.ernet.496248, pp. 244 to 246 and 251) | rank 1, the verse | **built** Kahala with the ninth lord; Matsya with the ninth alone; Lakshmi with the ninth lord in a kendra in its mooltrikona or at its deep exaltation, as the verse and the commentary read it; Kusuma with the Moon in the fifth, the commentary reading shubha as the ninth house, as v. 21 does, so a trikona other than it; each Hariharabrahma's grahas filling its houses one to a house in any order; Malika from every house, the fifth on from the Chowkhamba print; Chamara, Sankha, Srinatha and Khadga with BPHS's conditions, a test holding them to BPHS's answers. Nothing in the passage is left unbuilt |
| C482 | What Jataka Parijata ch. 7 vv. 1 to 127 leave to the reader | The raja yogas, the Moon's and the Sun's yogas, poverty and its cancellations. v. 2's first clause puts grahas in the third, fifth and fourth with no number; v. 3's benefics and malefics and v. 4's strong benefics are plural over houses; v. 6 asks Venus in Ashwini rising aspected by all the others, which the Sun and Mercury, never seventh from Venus, cannot do; v. 10's great friend is the compound relation, and its strong lagna names no lord; v. 11 has a manuscript reading that swaps benefics and malefics, given in the translation's note; v. 15's second figure, v. 19's uttamavarga and v. 25's pushkara navamsha name vargas the language does not carry or the verse does not fix; v. 21's three wealth yogas leave open which pair goes with which relation and whether the second lord must join; v. 32's malefics bereft of strength; v. 34 places the Sun, Venus and Mercury respectively third, sixth and eighth from the Moon, which the sky forbids; v. 35's friend's navamsha; v. 38's chief strength; v. 44's dual, Saturn and Mars, over three houses; v. 70's Sun and Mars in the third, from the Moon in the translation and from the lagna in the Chowkhamba Hindi; v. 75's malefics in the kendras; v. 76's benefic defeated in planetary war; v. 79's Moon bereft of strength; and vv. 80 and 81, which cancel poverty | V. Subrahmanya Sastri's 1932 translation, rendered off the page images (pp. 372 to 452), which lacks pp. 396 to 399 (vv. 23 to 28) in both its uploads; the Chowkhamba print with Kapileshvara Shastri's commentary and a Hindi gloss (archive.org dli.ernet.496248, pp. 194 to 242); every rule held to the computed sky, each that it never held given a fixture from a day-by-day search of 1800 to 2398 or a reason in UNSEEN | rank 1, the verse | **built** 106 rules: every one of the seven in v. 1's and v. 2's signs; one to a house in v. 2's seven houses; a graha at least of each kind in each house v. 3 names, and one strong benefic in a kendra for v. 4; v. 6 with three aspects, as Phaladeepika ch. 7 v. 8 reads the verse; v. 10 with the lagna lord strong; v. 11 as the verse stands; v. 32 with the Sun, Mars and Saturn weak, a waning Moon not asked to be, her Shadbala almost never falling short, and no instant searched holding all three weak, so it is listed unseen with the measure; v. 34 with each of the three in the third, sixth or eighth; v. 35 with each navamsha's lord a natural friend; v. 38's Moon strong and in her own sign or exaltation; v. 44 with each in one of the houses; v. 70 from the Moon; v. 79's Moon waning; Lagnadhi with the seven's aspects, the nodes' with theirs leaving no sixth, seventh and eighth all free. **Not built**: v. 2's first clause, v. 10's first, v. 11's second, vv. 15 and 19's first figures, v. 20's shashtyamsha, v. 21, v. 25's pushkara navamsha, v. 56, v. 75's second, v. 76's first and vv. 80 and 81, each needing a reading the verse does not give or a predicate the language lacks; vv. 86 to 107 and 121 to 124, Sunapha to Ubhayachari graha by graha, which ship from Saravali chs. 13 and 14 and BPHS ch. 38 |
| C483 | What Brihat Jataka ch. 12 v. 5's Vajra and Yava ask | v. 5 forms Vajra with the benefics placed as Sakata places the grahas, in the lagna and the seventh, and the malefics as Vihaga (Andaja) places them, in the fourth and the tenth, and Yava reversed; Sakata and Vihaga put all the seven in two houses, so the figure puts all the seven in the four kendras. v. 6 says the author states it after the older texts and asks how Mercury and Venus could stand fourth from the Sun. Measured with pyswisseph (Moshier, Lahiri), daily 1800 to 2398: Venus's greatest elongation is 47.28°, at most two signs from the Sun, and Mercury's 27.79°, at most one. Venus is a benefic on every reading and the Sun a malefic, so neither figure can hold. BPHS ch. 35 vv. 9 to 11 ask only a benefic or a malefic in each of the four houses, the rest anywhere, which can | Iyer's note quoting a local astrologer who reads the figures in the bhava chalit, where the fourth bhava can fall on a sign Venus reaches | rank 1, the verse and the author's own remark | **built** as all the seven in the kendras, each holding one, the benefics only in the lagna and the seventh and the malefics only in the fourth and the tenth (`BJ_NABHASA_VAJRA`, `BJ_NABHASA_YAVA`): unseen, the sky forbidding Venus three signs from the Sun, as v. 6 says; a test holds that neither answers on any recorded chart |
| C484 | How many kendras Brihat Jataka ch. 12 v. 2's dala yogas fill | v. 2 gives Parashara's Srak and Sarpa as the kendras joined by benefics and by malefics, with no count; Iyer's notes give Garga's benefics (Mercury, Jupiter and Venus) and malefics (the Sun, Mars and Saturn) and Badarayana's three kendras with none of the other kind in any kendra; BPHS ch. 35 v. 8 asks three kendras each with a benefic or a malefic and lets the other kind stand there, so that both can hold at once | a verse that counts the kendras | rank 1, the verse silent on the count | **built** as three kendras or more each holding a benefic, or a malefic, of the seven, by the readings' classes, and none of the other kind among the seven in any kendra, so the two exclude each other as the verse sets them against each other; the count is BPHS's |
| C485 | Which Nabhasa yoga stands where Brihat Jataka ch. 12's yogas mix | v. 10 sets the seven sankhya yogas aside for "the others stated before", the ashraya, dala and akriti yogas; v. 12 makes an ashraya yoga fruitless where another is mixed with it, that one giving the result. All the seven in one sign is always Rajju, Musala or Nala, so v. 10 taken whole leaves Gola on no sky, and v. 12 taken whole, every chart holding some sankhya yoga, leaves no ashraya; C448 settled BPHS's pack without the sankhya in the ashraya cancellation. Iyer relays the commentator: Kedara, Sula and Yuga mixed with an ashraya yoga are the ashraya, Gola mixed with one is Gola | a verse that orders the two clauses | rank 1, two verses that cannot both hold whole | **built**: Rajju, Musala and Nala cancelled by the two dala yogas, the twenty akriti yogas and Gola; Yuga to Vallaki cancelled by the three ashraya, two dala and twenty akriti yogas; Gola by the dala and akriti yogas alone, which no sky of one sign holds. The cancellations name this chapter's own yogas, whose Srak, Sarpa, Vajra and Yava are not BPHS's (C483, C484), so they are not twins of BPHS's; the twenty akriti figures otherwise are, eighteen of them twins of `NABHASA_*`. Gola holds on skies of 1800 to 2400 (1821 the first found) |
| C486 | What Brihat Jataka ch. 13 v. 3 makes Kemadruma | v. 3 names Sunapha, Anapha and Durudhara by grahas other than the Sun in the second from the Moon, the twelfth and both, respectively, and Kemadruma otherwise, "by many"; it reports that others refuse Kemadruma when the Moon is in a kendra or joined by a graha, and that some count the three from the kendras or from her navamsha sign, which it calls not established. v. 4 counts thirty-one Sunapha and Anapha each and a hundred and eighty Durudhara, every set of five grahas, so the grahas are Mars to Saturn. BPHS ch. 37 vv. 11 to 13 add a graha with the Moon and in a kendra from the lagna to what breaks Kemadruma, and Phaladeepika ch. 6 v. 5 ships the others' cancellation | whether the author adopts the others' cancellation, which v. 3 neither endorses nor rejects | rank 1, the verse | **built** Kemadruma as the absence of the three over Mars to Saturn with no cancellation, so exactly one of the four holds on every chart, which a test holds; the others' cancellation and the kendra and navamsha counts are not built. Sunapha, Anapha and Durudhara are twins of Phaladeepika's, BPHS's being inclusive |
| C487 | What Brihat Jataka ch. 13 v. 8's halves of the sky are | v. 8 makes the Moon harmful by day where she is seen and favourable where she is not, and the reverse otherwise, which Iyer's note reads as by night; the visible half is above the horizon, which whole signs from the lagna's own only approach, the lagna's sign and the seventh's each standing partly on both sides | a reading by the horizon's degree | rank 1, the verse | **built** as the seventh to the twelfth houses, whole signs, and the night as a birth not by day; on a chart that does not say whether it was by day, the night's branch answers, as Jataka Parijata ch. 7's night rules do |
| C488 | What Brihat Jataka ch. 13 v. 9 grades | v. 9 makes a native exceedingly wealthy with all the benefics in the upachayas from the lagna, wealthy with them all from the Moon, middling with two and of little wealth with fewer, without saying from which the two and the one are counted, and lets the yoga's result override other evil yogas, Kemadruma among them in Iyer's note. BPHS ch. 37 v. 6 grades the three counts from the Moon alone | which reference the two and the one take | rank 1, the verse | **built** all, two and one from each reference, six rules, the three from the Moon twins of BPHS's; three is the benefics' count by the readings' classes, and from the lagna the Moon is not counted. The override is not built: it weighs one result against another and names no condition |
| C489 | What Brihat Jataka ch. 13 vv. 1, 2 and 7 leave to the reader | v. 1's great friend's navamsha is the compound relation; v. 2's benefics in the sixth, seventh and eighth from the Moon, which Iyer's note makes seven sorts, any one of the houses or more, and BPHS ch. 37 v. 5 one in each; v. 7 gives each graha making Sunapha, Anapha or Durudhara its own result without saying which yoga | a verse that counts v. 2's houses | rank 1, the verse | **built** v. 1 as Jataka Parijata ch. 7 v. 112, which restates it, a twin; v. 2 as BPHS's Adhi yoga, a twin, the seven sorts not built; v. 7 as the graha in the second or the twelfth from the Moon, whichever of the three it makes |
| C490 | What Brihat Jataka ch. 15's pravrajya yogas leave to the reader | v. 1's four or more "endowed with strength" in one sign, the strongest giving the order, pairs the orders with the grahas otherwise than BPHS ch. 79 does (Mars the Shakya, Mercury the Ajivika, Jupiter the Bhikshu, the Moon the Vriddhashravaka, Venus the Charaka, Saturn the Nirgrantha, the Sun the Vanyashana); its second line and v. 2's second line turn on the leader beaten in planetary war; v. 2's first line makes a combust leader leave the native devoted but uninitiated, where BPHS only cancels; v. 3's lord of the Moon's sign is "unseen by others" without saying whether Saturn, whom it aspects, is one of them, and its second figure has Saturn aspect the weak lord without BPHS v. 7's "alone"; v. 3's Moon asks Saturn's drekkana and a navamsha of Mars or Saturn together, where BPHS v. 8 takes either; v. 4's two figures ask the native born "in a yoga of kings", naming none; Iyer's notes add the order of each strong graha in its dasha, the Moon unaspected by others in v. 3, and an ascetic without the raja yoga in v. 4 | N. Chidambaram Iyer's 1885 translation, the Sanskrit of vv. 1 to 4 read beside it; ch. 14's pairs read again against the Sanskrit | rank 1, the verse | **built** 19 rules, `BJ_PRAVRAJYA_*`: v. 1 with BPHS ch. 79 vv. 2 and 3's conditions and Varahamihira's orders, a combust leader cancelling it, so the seven answer what BPHS's do; v. 2's combust leader as six rules of its own, the Sun never combust; v. 3's first figure as BPHS v. 6 reads "others", every graha, which leaves the lord only Mars's eighth or Jupiter's fifth or ninth, aspects Saturn does not return, the two strengths as two rules answering BPHS's; its second without "alone"; its Moon with both divisions, a drekkana of Saturn read as Capricorn or Aquarius in the decanate chart; v. 4's raja yoga as one of ch. 11's 30 rules holding, named by key. **Not built**: the planetary war of vv. 1 and 2, whose victor the chart does not carry; the notes' dasha sequence, unaspected Moon and ascetic without a raja yoga, which are the commentary's. | C532 | What Saravali ch. 39 vv. 21 and 22 leave unsaid: the minutes of the navamsha, and whose figure v. 22 is | v. 21 places Pisces rising in its last navamsha with 25 minutes named in it, Mercury, Jupiter and Venus in Taurus and "the rest" exalted, the Moon among them; v. 22 is Brihat Jataka ch. 7 v. 14's and BPHS ch. 43 v. 55's Cancer figure; vv. 1 to 20 compute the span of life and are no yoga | Saravali ch. 39 (1907 print); Brihat Jataka ch. 7 v. 14; BPHS ch. 43 v. 55 | rank 1, the verses | **built** 2 rules: v. 21 as a long life, Pisces 26°40′ to 30° rising, the minutes not asked; v. 22 as Brihat Jataka's rule (twin, also equal to BPHS's). **Not built**: vv. 1 to 20, the computation of the span |
| C533 | Whether Saravali ch. 44's counts are "exactly" or "at least", and why the exalted and debilitated counts stop at six | vv. 23 to 45 read two to seven grahas exalted, in moolatrikona, own, friendly, debilitated and hostile signs, each count in turn with its own reading, which only an exact count keeps apart; seven exalted cannot be (the Sun in Aries and Mercury in Virgo stand 150° apart) nor seven debilitated (the Sun in Libra and Mercury in Pisces); vv. 1 to 22 read one graha's dignity at a time | Saravali ch. 44 (1907 print); Jataka Parijata ch. 8 vv. 106 to 116 | rank 1, the verses | **built** 34 rules, each an exact count over the seven by the SDK's dignity ladder, the moolatrikona span first; friend and enemy by the compound relation, the friend counts two to seven and enemy counts five to seven equal to Jataka Parijata ch. 8's (twins). **Not built**: vv. 1 to 22, single-graha readings; seven exalted and seven debilitated, which the sky forbids |

v. 7 malefics in the fifth and ninth, each aspected by a malefic; v. 10 Mars joined with the waning Moon; v. 14 the Moon waning; v. 15 the Sun in a sign of Mars. v. 16 is Brihat Jataka's rule, which the sky forbids under the print and its variant of three houses; it ships unseen. **Not built**: v. 2 (an empty eighth, unclear), v. 13b (garbled in the print), v. 20 (places), vv. 22 to 45 (the drekkana causes, v. 23's malefic clause of uncertain reach) |
| C540 | Which of Uttara Kalamrita's verses state a yoga, and at what rank | Kalidasa's book has two kandas. Kanda I has eight sections: I to III define the chart, measure strength and give the length of life; IV, 45 verses, holds the yogas and the verses that judge a house; V lists what each house and graha signifies; VI gives the dashas, VII queries and VIII the muhurta chakras. Kanda II's first 76 verses are ritual, and vv. 77 to 105 rate lords by lagna, the nodes and the marakas, with a few yogas among them. Three English translations were read: V. Subrahmanya Sastri's, in a 2012 transcription that drops articles and interleaves his notes; P. S. Sastri's (Ranjan); and S. S. Sareen's (Sagar, 1993). The Sanskrit OCR in the last two cannot be read | the Sanskrit, read clean | rank 1: a classical text in translation, which is how CLEAN_ROOM lists it | **built** 123 rules, `UTTARA_*`, with chapter `I.4` or `II`: I.4 vv. 3 to 8, 10, 11, 13, 22, 23, 26 to 29, 35, 37, 39 and 42 to 45, and II vv. 79, 82, 83, 89, 90, 92 to 96, 99 to 101, 104 and 105. Two restate another text's yoga and copy its conditions: v. 3's exchange is Phaladeepika ch. 7 v. 9's, and v. 26 is Jataka Parijata ch. 7 v. 113's Chandradhi. **Not built**: definitions and tables the SDK computes or reads elsewhere (I.4 vv. 1, 2, 15 to 21, 24, 25 and 31 to 34); placements read one graha at a time (vv. 9, 14, 16 and 38, and II v. 103's second half); section V's significations; the dashas, marakas and timing (section VI, II vv. 84 to 88 and 97); queries and muhurta (VII, VIII); the length of life (section III, II v. 100's first half, v. 102 and v. 103's first half); v. 41's siblings counted by navamshas; II v. 101's brothers, whose houses name no reference, and its corrupt end; and the lagnas' lists of good and bad lords (II vv. 79 to 81 and 90 to 96, apart from the pairs they name), which rate a graha and state no yoga |
| C541 | How Uttara Kalamrita I.4 relates a pair of lords | vv. 3, 4, 5, 23 and 28 name three relations: being together, exchanging signs and aspecting each other. v. 3's clause (e) joins the ninth and tenth lords to "any of" the lords of the 5th, 7th, 1st and 4th, without saying whether both lords join or one. v. 4's third alternative joins "the pair" to the lord of the sign the lagna lord stands in. v. 4's pairs counted from the ninth and the tenth are the fifth and sixth lords and the sixth and seventh. v. 23's malefics and benefics are plurals with no count. For a Taurus lagna one graha, Saturn, lords both the ninth and the tenth | P. S. Sastri's and Sareen's renderings, which agree on the three relations | rank 1, the verse | **built** being together as `lord-conjunct-lord`, so one graha lording both houses counts as the pair joined; exchange as `mutual-exchange`; and mutual aspect as each graha aspecting the other. v. 3's (e) is built as the two lords together with a lord of the 1st, 4th, 5th or 7th that is neither of them; its proviso as neither lord also owning the 8th or 11th. v. 4's third alternative is built as both lords in that dispositor's sign. Plurals are read as one or more |
| C542 | What the four sets of Uttara Kalamrita I.4 v. 5 are | The translations name set (a) three ways. V. S. Sastri: the lords of the sign and navamsha a graha occupies, and the lagna lord. Sareen: the Moon's sign lord and the lagna navamsha's lord. P. S. Sastri: the lords of the lagna, of the navamsha lagna, and of the sign the lagna lord stands in. Set (c), the lords of the 5th, 3rd, 4th and 7th, has four members and no count. In V. S. and P. S. Sastri the dignity attaches to the mutual aspect alone | the three translations | rank 1, translations that part | **built** sets (b), (c) and (d). Each set holds when two of its lords are together in one of the set's houses, have exchanged signs, or aspect each other from exalted, own or friendly signs; set (c) is read as any two of its four lords. **Not built**: set (a) |
| C543 | How Uttara Kalamrita I.4 vv. 6 and 10 to 13 judge a house | The verses are said of every house, "the lagna and the others". v. 6 counts the eighth from the house itself: "therefrom" in V. S. Sastri, "from the lagna or from it" in Sareen. v. 10 names the dusthanas without saying where they are counted from, joins its afflictions with "or", and includes defeat in planetary war. v. 11 joins its lord's place, its dignity, its hemming by strong benefics and an exalted dispositor with "and" in V. S. Sastri; Sareen joins the hemming with "or". v. 11 also offers, as another alternative, the lord joined or aspected by a benefic. vv. 12 and 18 need each house's karaka | V. S. Sastri, Sareen | rank 1, the verse | **built** twelve rules for each of vv. 6, 10, 11 and 13, one per house, keyed `UTTARA_BHAVA_<house>_*`. v. 6 counts the eighth from the house. v. 10 counts the dusthanas from the lagna, as Phaladeepika ch. 6 does, and any one affliction is enough; both verses also require that no benefic joins or aspects the lord. v. 11's first alternative is built with every clause, the strong benefics in the second and twelfth from the lord. v. 13 requires the lords of the lord's sign and of its navamsha to be strong. **Not built**: v. 11's second alternative, which alone holds on most charts (C470's precedent); v. 10's planetary war, which the language cannot express; vv. 12 and 18, since the book gives no karaka for each house in a form a rule can name |
| C544 | Which varga is Uttara Kalamrita I.4 v. 7's "varga of a benefic" | The lagna lord in the lagna, in any of its drekkanas, makes an emperor when Venus, Jupiter or Mercury joins or aspects it, when it stands in "the varga of a benefic", or when it is exalted. The verse names no varga | the three translations, which agree | rank 1, the verse | **built** the varga as the navamsha, in a sign of Venus, Jupiter or Mercury, the three benefics the verse names; the drekkanas as the lagna lord's ten-degree thirds of the lagna sign |
| C545 | How Uttara Kalamrita I.4 v. 22 reads the lords of the sixth, eighth and twelfth | V. S. (d) is built as each of the three lords related to the other two (together, exchanged or in mutual aspect), and none of the other seven so related to any of them |

Its malefic figures (malefics in the arudha lagna, its kendras and trikonas, or in the 6th, 8th and 12th from it) hold whenever one malefic stands in one of those houses, which is nearly every chart. v. 37's first figure joins Mercury, Saturn and Mars to the lords of "the three upapadas" and to "rasis and amsas", which the translations read differently. v. 37's adopted sons are read from the tenth. v. 39 reads the "upapada of the seventh" | V. S. Sastri's note and v. 34's definition; Sareen | rank 1, the verse and its note | **built** v. 35's two benefic figures, and v. 37's Saturn with Jupiter and its adopted sons from the tenth, as printed. v. 39's upapada is built as the pada of the sixth: v. 34 calls the pada of the house before a house its upapada, and the translator's note and example read it so. **Not built**: v. 35's malefic figures and v. 37's first figure |

| C548 | What Uttara Kalamrita II vv. 104 and 105 lack | v. 104 lacks two lines, and asks for a nakshatra other than Chitra where Jataka Parijata ch. 3 v. 57 asks for the tripada stars. v. 105 is in another metre, the translator reads it as an insertion, and it speaks of a couple's charts | V. S. Sastri's notes | rank 1, a corrupt verse | **built** both as printed, v. 105 on the native's own chart |
| C549 | The Uttara Kalamrita I.4 figures no sky holds or the language cannot express | v. 42 puts the full Moon and Venus with the adhikagraha. Venus is never more than 47° from the Sun, and the full Moon (Shukla Ekadashi to Krishna Panchami) is at least 120° from him, so the two never share a sign; the sky search of 1800 to 2398 found no instance. v. 30 times a birth two ghatikas after midnight or midday, which no limb measures, and its second half applies only "in the above case". v. 29 also grades its figure by strength | the sky search; the language | rank 1, the verse | **not built**: v. 42's figure, which all three translations read as the full Moon and Venus together; v. 30 as a whole; v. 29's grading |
| C550 | How BPHS chs. 12 to 23 were read, and the conventions every rule of them shares | The twelve house chapters were read in the Sanskrit alone, as sanskritdocuments.org transcribes the text (Ahto Jarve, proofread by Ginda Lass), whose verse numbers the rules cite; R. Santhanam's translation, which the other BPHS rules follow, is not on hand, and the two can number a verse apart (ch. 83's first serpent curse is v. 8 in the shipped rule and v. 9 in the transcription). The chapters leave five things to the reader throughout: a clause with no subject ("joined by Jupiter", "aspected by benefics") after two grahas are named; plurals of benefics and malefics with no count; a "trine from" a graha; the deep exaltation (parama-uccha), which the SDK's dignity did not report until these chapters asked it; and the karaka of a house, which ch. 32 v. 34 lists (the Sun, Jupiter, Mars, the Moon, Jupiter, Mars, Venus, Saturn, Jupiter, Mercury, Jupiter and Saturn for the first to the twelfth) | Santhanam's numbering and wording for chs. 12 to 23 | rank 1, the verse | **built** 234 rules over the conventions: a subjectless clause read of the graha last named; a plural as one at least, unless a verse gives a number; a trine from a graha as the fifth and ninth from it; the deep exaltation as the `DEEP_EXALTED` dignity, within a degree of BPHS ch. 3's point as a deep debilitation is (C18); the karaka as ch. 32 v. 34 lists it. Each rule cites its verse in the transcription's numbering. **Open**: checking the numbering and the readings against Santhanam |
| C551 | What BPHS ch. 12, the first house, leaves unsaid | v. 2's benefics in kendras and trikonas "remove every disease" with no count, after the first line's lagna lord combust, debilitated or hostile; v. 3's lagne candre is the lagna or the Moon, not the Moon in the lagna; v. 7's Mercury, Jupiter or Venus "in the lagna with the Moon, or in a kendra from the lagna" could drop the Moon from the kendras; v. 11 says the same results are to be read from the Moon; vv. 12 to 15 map each house and its decanate to a limb and mark a wound where a malefic stands, a mole with Mercury | the Sanskrit | rank 1, the verse | **built** 14 rules: v. 2's benefics as one at least, a rule of its own that cancels the first line's; v. 3 as the lagna or the Moon; v. 7 with the Moon's company in the lagna and the kendras alike; v. 4's benefic and malefic in the lagna with Jataka Parijata ch. 7 v. 125's Shubha and Ashubha, v. 8 with Brihat Jataka ch. 5 v. 5's cord, v. 9 with its v. 4's twins, all copied. **Not built**: v. 11's reading from the Moon, which names no figure; vv. 12 to 15's limbs, a table of readings by placement rather than a yoga |
| C552 | What BPHS ch. 13, the second house, leaves unsaid | v. 4 joins the second lord in the eleventh, the eleventh lord in the second and both in kendras or trikonas, the last by tāvubhau with no 'or'; v. 5's eleventh lord "in its trine" names no reference, and its Jupiter and Venus no one they join or aspect; v. 6's lords "joined or aspected by malefics" give no count; v. 11's pārāvatāṃśa is a vaiśeṣikāṃśa of the sixteen vargas, which the verse does not define; v. 12's lord of the eyes is the second lord | the Sanskrit | rank 1, the verse | **built** 14 rules: v. 4 as three alternatives; v. 5 with the eleventh lord in the fifth or ninth from the second lord, joined or aspected by Jupiter and by Venus; v. 6 with each lord touched by one malefic; v. 12's lord in a dusthana with v. 2's, one rule, copying Phaladeepika ch. 6's Nissva; v. 2's benefic in the second copying Saravali ch. 34's. **Not built**: v. 11, its amsha undefined |
