| C554 | What BPHS ch. 15, the fourth house, leaves unsaid | v. 6's karaka of the mother is the Moon by ch. 32 vv. 22 and 34, where vehicles (v. 10's karaka) would ask Venus; v. 9's first line, caragehasamāyukto sukhe tadrāśināyake, names a movable sign and the fourth's lord without saying which stands where; v. 10 joins a benefic lagna lord, a debilitated fourth lord, the karaka in the twelfth and the fourth lord in the eleventh to a vehicle in the twelfth year, which v. 13 gives to the fourth and eleventh lords exchanged | the Sanskrit | rank 1, the verse | **built** 11 rules: v. 6 with the Moon strong; v. 9 as the fourth lord in a movable sign; v. 13 as the exchange. **Not built**: v. 10, whose clauses cannot be told apart from v. 13's or given one karaka |
| C555 | What BPHS ch. 16, the fifth house, leaves unsaid | v. 1's "or in a kendra or trikona" is the fifth lord's or both lords'; v. 6's yatra and tatra put Saturn and Mercury where the debilitated fifth lord stands; v. 8, ṣaṣṭhāṣṭamavyayastho vā nīco vā śatrurāśigaḥ sutesaś ca sute, names a graha in a dusthana, debilitated or hostile, and the fifth lord in the fifth, without saying whose; v. 9's Saturn and Mandi "joined, aspected" give Mandi an aspect a point does not cast; v. 16's dvitrikoṇa is the second and a trine or the two trines; v. 20's "or" may join Venus's two places or the whole figure; vv. 29 to 31 ask Jupiter in the fifth from Saturn and Saturn in the fifth from Jupiter, which cannot hold at once | the Sanskrit | rank 1, the verse | **built** 29 rules: v. 1 of the fifth lord's place; v. 6 with Saturn and Mercury beside the lord; v. 9 with Saturn and Mandi together and Saturn in or aspecting the fifth; v. 16 as the fifth and ninth; v. 20 as Venus's place; v. 29 with either as alternatives; v. 2's fifth lord in a dusthana copying Phaladeepika ch. 6's Pamara; v. 10 shipping under ch. 12 v. 10, which it repeats. v. 11's six grahas in the fifth, its lord in the twelfth, was found twice in a day-by-day search of 1800 to 2398. **Not built**: v. 8, its subject unread |

| C595 | Whether Brihat Jataka ch. 21 v. 1's counts are "exactly" or "at least", and what an own house and a friend's sign are | v. 1 reads one to seven grahas in their own houses, then one to seven in friendly signs, each count with its own effect rising a step at a time (the family's common standing up to a king; living on others up to a king); it does not say whether the moolatrikona or Mercury's exaltation in Virgo, both inside a graha's own sign, count as own, nor whose friendship makes a sign friendly | Iyer's 1885 translation, read off the page images (pp. 186 and 187); Jataka Parijata ch. 8 vv. 106 and 109; Saravali ch. 44 (C533) | rank 1, the verse | **built** 14 rules, each an exact count over the seven, since only an exact count keeps the steps apart; an own house as a sign the graha rules, whatever its place on the dignity ladder (a graha its own sign's lord), so the moolatrikona and exalted Mercury in Virgo are counted and the Moon's moolatrikona in Taurus is not; a friend's sign by the compound relation, as Jataka Parijata ch. 8 v. 109 counts it, whose seven rules are its twins. Jataka Parijata ch. 8 v. 106's own counts take the moolatrikona dignity and the own-sign dignity, which counts the Moon in Taurus and leaves out Mercury in Virgo outside 16° to 20°, so they are not its twins. Seven in their own houses held on no day from 1800 to 2398: the Sun in Leo, Mercury in Virgo and Venus in Libra together on 868 days, with Jupiter and Saturn at home on 41, and with Mars in Aries or Scorpio on none; from 1001 BCE to 1800, 8 days had the six but the Moon and none the seven. It ships as unseen, which the sky does not forbid but has not shown in 3,400 years |
| C596 | What Brihat Jataka ch. 21 v. 2 leaves unsaid | the first half reads a single graha exalted and aspected by a friend, or joined by one; the second half reads one to seven grahas in an enemy's sign or their debilitation, each count with its own effect. The verse does not say whether "one" means one only, which friendship it means, or whether "exaltation" is the sign or the dignity. Iyer's note says the seven cannot all be debilitated, since the Sun in Libra and Mercury in Pisces stand 150° apart, and calls the verse a quotation of earlier writers | Iyer's 1885 translation and its note; Jataka Parijata ch. 8 v. 104 | rank 1, the verse, with the translator's note | **built** the exalted graha as one or more, in its exaltation sign as a whole sign (the dignity ladder reads the Moon at 4° to 20° Taurus and Mercury at 16° to 20° Virgo as moolatrikona); the friend by natural relation, the exalted graha's regard for it, as Jataka Parijata ch. 8 v. 104 reads it. That rule asks the exalted dignity, so the two are not twins. The enemy-or-debilitated counts are exact and by the compound relation. Iyer's note bears on debilitation alone, while the verse takes enemy signs too: seven in an enemy's sign or debilitated held on the computed sky (3 of 3 asked from 1800), so v. 2's seventh count ships |
| C597 | What Brihat Jataka ch. 21 vv. 3 to 5 leave unsaid | v. 3 sets Satya's Aquarius rising beside the Yavanas' Aquarius dwadashamsha rising, and Vishnugupta's objection that every sign holds such a dwadashamsha and that several signs are given good effects; it gives no verdict. v. 4 puts "the malefic planets" in the Sun's horas of odd signs and "the benefic planets" in the Moon's horas of even signs, with no count. v. 5 gives the effects in moderate measure when the horas are as stated and the signs are not, and none when both differ; Iyer's N. B. adds the malefics in the Moon's horas of odd signs and the benefics in the Sun's of even signs as moderate too, a case the verse does not name | Iyer's 1885 translation and its notes (a), (b) and N. B. | rank 1, the verse | **built** both readings of v. 3 as rules, each note naming the dispute; the horas as ch. 1 gives them (an odd sign's first half the Sun's, an even sign's the Moon's); "the malefics" and "the benefics" as every one of the seven the readings class so, none outside the hora asked; v. 5 as every one in a hora of the right lord and not every one in a sign of the right parity, so a mix of signs is moderate. **Not built**: the failing case, which names no effect, and Iyer's N. B., which is his note and not the verse |
| C598 | Which drekkanas Brihat Jataka ch. 21 v. 6 calls friendly, serpent, armed, quadruped and bird | the verse gives the Moon in her own or a friend's drekkana fine beauty, and in another drekkana effects after that drekkana's lord; then wickedness in a serpent drekkana, cruelty in an armed one, the preceptor's wife in a quadruped one and wandering in a bird one. Iyer's note (a) makes the friend one "at the time of birth" and note (b) grades the lord neutral and hostile. His notes (c) to (f) list the kinds: serpent Cancer's second, Scorpio's first and Pisces's third; armed Aries's first and third, Gemini's second, Leo's first, Libra's second and Aquarius's first; quadruped Aries's second, Taurus's second and third, Cancer's first, Libra's third, Scorpio's third and Sagittarius's first; bird Aries's second, Gemini's second, Leo's first and Libra's second. A drekkana in two lists gives both effects. His ch. 27 notes on the 36 figures agree on the birds, add Leo's first and third to the quadrupeds, put snakes in Cancer's third and Scorpio's second as well, and arm more figures (Gemini's third, Leo's second and third, Virgo's second, Sagittarius's first and third); his ch. 23 v. 16 note lists the serpents otherwise again (C526). | C733 | Ashauch: the Mitākṣarā's own table | Mit on Yā III.18 reads "ūnadvivarṣa ubhayoḥ" first as the parents' full period; its III.23 summary cuts the first three years by the cūḍā; its night is three kalpas by local usage (III.20) | which kalpa a family in Nepal keeps; a Nepal statement for the parents' period | 1 | built: `MITAKSHARA` with the parents' full period, bands filtered by `chudakarana`, the first kalpa (`HALF_NIGHT`); every row data a family can change |

## How to use this page

An implementer picks an item, reads the text, and closes it with a pull
request that adds the citation to the row and the fixture that proves it.
A reviewer for a tradition (GOVERNANCE.md) signs off on the closure. New
discrepancies found while reading a reference are added here first and
resolved second; the rule is that a question is recorded, never absorbed
into a table as if it were an answer.
